# M4-W2c curious controller with memory

Closed loop on the locked-rooms world. Bits are prequential, on observation tokens only.
PROBE_cap is the mean over rooms 0..8 of min(probe_k, 4). Seeds 15–20.

## Capability check

World seed 0, T = 20000, no learner. Reward 1 on the first step that ends in a room not entered before, else 0. Room 0 counts as entered at the start. Q updates every step.

Deepest room 1. First step 496.

First entry step by room. A hyphen means the room was not entered by a step. Room 0 is a hyphen because it is entered at the start: - 496 - - - - - - -.

## Protocol

- Rooms 0..8. Actions 0..5 manipulate, 6 is the TV, 7 is reset. Vocabulary 152.
- Closed loop, chunks of 32 actions. Q is frozen inside a chunk. Score, live Adam step, replay Adam step, then Q updates in order.
- lp_mem and lp_room use prog_lp. Context is (room before the action, action). K = 16. Visit n ≤ 32 rewards 1. Visit n > 32 rewards mean(bits at visits n−32..n−17) minus mean(bits at visits n−16..n−1). The current visit is not in the window. Its bits_before is appended after the reward.
- count_mem reward is 1/sqrt(n) on the memory state-action pair, n including the current visit.
- lp_mem and count_mem: state is (room, p1, p2). p1 is the previous action, p2 the one before. Each is 0..8, and 8 means none. A step that changes the room, including a reset that leaves the room, sets p1 and p2 to 8. A step that stays in the room sets p1 to that action and p2 to the old p1. A TV press stays in the room. Q is 729 by 8, zeros at the start.
- lp_room is M4-W2b prog_lp with room-only state. Q is 9 by 8. rand6 is M4-W2b rand6.
- ε = 0.1 over actions 0..5 only. Greedy argmax is over all 8 actions. Ties are uniform. α = 0.1, γ = 0.9. Controller rng is default_rng(7000 + world_seed).
- rand6 uses the W2-0 policy and rng. Learner seed 0. Replay seed 30000. Adam lr 0.001, clip 1.0, replay batch 8.
- GRU embedding 32, hidden 128, 1 layer, readout 152. 86680 parameters. CPU, 4 threads.
- Checkpoints T/4, T/2 and T. Probe boundaries are 15008, 30016 and 60000.
- PROBE_cap caps each room probe at 4 bits. Seeds 15, 16, 17, 18, 19 and 20.
- Kernels ezzzzzx/m4w2c-a, m4w2c-b and m4w2c-c. Private, CPU, no internet. Code dataset ezzzzzx/pdp-code-m4w2c.


## Verdict (orchestrator, 11 Nov 2026, UTC+8)

**Bar 1 FAIL, bar 2 PASS, bar 3 FAIL, and the kill rule fires.**
- Bar 1: rand6 − lp_mem = −1.69 ± 0.37 on PROBE_cap (bar ≥ +0.25). It is negative on all 6 seeds.
- Bar 2: count_mem − lp_mem = +0.32 ± 0.31 (bar ≥ +0.10). **Holds.** Progress beat count novelty.
- Bar 3: lp_room − lp_mem = +0.23 ± 0.60 (bar ≥ 1.0). Memory helped a little, not by much.
- lp_mem's TV share is 3.2%. The noise leak stays fixed.

**Mechanism: greedy tie-breaks press reset.** The capability check is decisive. With an ideal reward (+1 for entering
a room never entered before) and no learner, the memory controller reached only room 1 in 20,000 steps. Every new
memory state starts with Q = 0 on all 8 actions. Uniform tie-breaking over all 8 then presses reset one time in
eight, which is exactly rand8, the policy W2-0 showed never gets past room 2. Memory multiplies the number of fresh
states (729 instead of 9), so it multiplies the tie-break resets. The resets show it: lp_mem pressed reset on 3.5–8%
of steps, one every 12–28 steps, where a lock takes about 216 random manipulations. M4-W2's ε error has come back
through the greedy branch. I specified "ties uniform over all 8" in M4-W2b and M4-W2c without seeing this. My error.

What three runs show:
- In W2, an action that undoes progress (reset) dominates every result. A controller that ever presses it at random
  cannot go deep, whatever its reward. rand6 wins by never having it.
- The noise leak is solved by measuring progress visit to visit (TV share 2–3%).
- Learning progress beats count novelty when both have the same controller (bar 2 here).

As pre-registered, the tabular-controller line on W2 stops. A fair test of curiosity in W2 needs a controller whose
exploration treats reset as a deliberate, costly choice. A model-based planner that uses the learner's own predictions
is one.
